        If White plays `Qe4+` or something? No, the Queen is at h4.
        If White plays `Qa1+`? No.
        If White plays `Rh7`? No.
        White's Rook is at a7. It's attacking f7? No, it's on the a-file and 7th rank.
        Wait, a7 is the 7th rank. f7 is on the 7th rank!
        So the Rook at a7 is attacking the pawn at f7!
        If Black plays `g8f8`, the pawn at f7 is still attacked by the Rook at a7.
        If Black plays `f7f6` or `f7f5`, the pawn at f7 moves, so it's no longer attacked by the Rook at a7 (though it might be attacked at f6 or f5).

        Wait, if the Rook is at a7 and the pawn is at f7, the Rook is attacking f7.
        If I play `g8f8`, I capture the Bishop, but the Rook at a7 still attacks f7.
        If I play `f7f6`, I move the pawn.
        If I play `f7f5`, I move the pawn.

        Which is better?
        `g8f8` takes a piece.
        `f7f6`/`f7f5` just moves a pawn.

        Between `f7f6` and `f7f5`:
        `f7f5` is more aggressive.

        However, `g8f8` is a capture. In chess, usually, captures are priority unless it leads to immediate disaster.
        Is `g8f8` a disaster?
        After `g8f8`, White can play `Rxf7+`.
        Then the King must move (e.g., `f8e8` or `f8g8`).
        Wait, if `Rxf7+`, the Rook is now at f7.
        The King is at f8.
        The Rook at f7 is checking the King at f8.
        What are the escapes?
        `f8e8` (if legal), `f8g8`.
        But `Rxf7+` also puts the Rook on a very strong square.

        Actually, if I'm down to just a King and two pawns against a Queen and two Rooks, the game is realistically lost. The goal is to seek any possible counterplay or prolonged resistance.
        Taking the Bishop `g8f8` seems like the most proactive move.
